    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nThis section tests the three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}): trace correlation and $K_{\\text{eff}}$ collapse (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), systematic confirmation bias with $\\text{BR} > 1$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}), supporting diagnostics (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostics}), and the inescapability of the mirage under twenty-plus same-model interventions (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$, yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence alone, appears to influence the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ produces significant degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$; positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\nThe trace correlation above manifests through a specific mechanism that explains \\emph{all three} regime failures: self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\\times$ (pooled raw ratio for Mistral-7B FOLIO default seed; $4.24\\times$ when MaxSAT-weighted; per-model ratios in Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}) compared to correct problems. Consequently, 94\\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors: symbolic aggregation adds no independent corrective signal. The MAX-SAT solver rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints on average ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| / |\\mathcal{C}|$), with no significant difference between correct and incorrect predictions ($p = 0.636$), confirming that degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}) is the dominant regime.\n\nThe causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts FOL consistent with it. Constraints from majority-answer traces use trace-specific variable names (56.4\\% of cross-answer pairs share zero Z3 variables; Z3 operates over logical structure via alpha-equivalence, mitigating this syntactic confound), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all, and aggregation reduces to a weighted majority vote (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). Confirmation bias generalizes across model families: bias ratios of $4.97\\times$ (Mistral-7B), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen2.5-14B), and $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA-3.1-8B) all significantly exceed $1.0$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). The bias ratio gradient aligns with regime boundaries: high bias (Mistral, $4.24\\times$ weighted) at intermediate SC produces SICA's only positive trends; low bias (LLaMA, $2.66\\times$) at high SC produces the worst degradation ($-4.41$\\,pp), because low confirmation bias at high SC means the model's consensus is already near-correct and constraints provide no leverage. The oracle ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms that ${\\sim}$88\\% of the same-model potential gain is lost to self-extraction quality.\n\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nAnswer-level $\\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.41$. The process--answer correlation is model-strength dependent: $r = -0.06$ ($p = 0.36$) on Mistral-7B, where answer diversity does not reflect reasoning diversity (Figure~\\ref{fig:process-kappa}), rising to $r = +0.24$ ($p < 0.001$) on Qwen2.5-14B. This pattern generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the cosine--$\\kappa$ gap narrows from $0.23$ (Mistral) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), meaning answer-level $\\kappa$ already captures the bottleneck for strong models, while process-$\\kappa$ reveals hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. In all families, interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_process_kappa_scatter.pdf}\n\\vspace{-2mm}\n\\caption{Answer agreement vs.\\ CoT cosine similarity (Mistral-7B, FOLIO, $n{=}204$). Flat regression ($r = -0.06$, $p = 0.36$); stronger models show significant positive correlation ($r = +0.24$, Qwen-14B; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}).}\n\\label{fig:process-kappa}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics---constraint ablation, discriminativeness, entropy decomposition, and hyperparameter sensitivity---confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nWe test more than twenty remediation strategies spanning prompt engineering, method-level redesign, and ten additional directions. All produce neutral or negative results.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-level interventions.} Nine strategies spanning six dimensions (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:remediation}) all fail: contrastive prompting reduces non-discriminative constraints but $\\Delta = -1.1$\\,pp; cross-model extraction yields accuracy within $\\pm 1$\\,pp; multi-temperature ($T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$) yields $\\Delta = 0.00$\\,pp.\n\n\\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} ICG ($\\Delta \\approx 0$, $p = .206$), Contrastive ($\\Delta = -9.31$\\,pp, $p < 0.01$), and Debate ($\\Delta = -18.63$/$-23.00$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$) all fail; three of six conditions reach significance, all negative (Table~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, Appendix~\\ref{app:remediation}).\n\n\\paragraph{Additional directions.} Ten further experiments---code verification, iterative refinement, logprob filtering, cross-model ensembles, alternative objectives---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:additional_interventions}).\n\n\\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} (Appendix) compares $\\kappa$ across four aggregation methods and three model families. SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$; Debate reaches $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp. All families cluster above $\\kappa = 0.40$, confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa.",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nThis section tests the three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}): trace correlation and $K_{\\text{eff}}$ collapse (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), systematic confirmation bias with $\\text{BR} > 1$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}), supporting diagnostics (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostics}), and the inescapability of the mirage under twenty-plus same-model interventions (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$, yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence alone, appears to influence the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ produces significant degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$; positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\nThe trace correlation above manifests through a specific mechanism that explains \\emph{all three} regime failures: self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\\times$ (pooled raw ratio for Mistral-7B FOLIO default seed; $4.24\\times$ when MaxSAT-weighted; per-model ratios in Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}) compared to correct problems. Consequently, 94\\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors: symbolic aggregation adds no independent corrective signal. The MAX-SAT solver rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints on average ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| / |\\mathcal{C}|$, $p = 0.636$ between correct/incorrect), confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). Counterintuitively, jointly-wrong problems produce \\emph{more} constraints (41.9 vs.\\ 37.1 for correct): higher symbolic density amplifies rather than corrects bias.\n\nThe causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts FOL consistent with it. Constraints from majority-answer traces use trace-specific variable names (56.4\\% of cross-answer pairs share zero Z3 variables; Z3 operates over logical structure via alpha-equivalence, mitigating this syntactic confound), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all, and aggregation reduces to a weighted majority vote (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). Confirmation bias generalizes across model families: bias ratios of $4.97\\times$ (Mistral-7B), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen2.5-14B), and $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA-3.1-8B) all significantly exceed $1.0$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). The bias ratio gradient aligns with regime boundaries: high bias (Mistral, $4.24\\times$ weighted) at intermediate SC produces SICA's only positive trends; low bias (LLaMA, $2.66\\times$) at high SC produces the worst degradation ($-4.41$\\,pp), because low confirmation bias at high SC means the model's consensus is already near-correct and constraints provide no leverage. The oracle ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms that ${\\sim}$88\\% of the same-model potential gain is lost to self-extraction quality.\n\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nAnswer-level $\\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.41$. The process--answer correlation is model-strength dependent: $r = -0.06$ ($p = 0.36$) on Mistral-7B, where answer diversity does not reflect reasoning diversity (Figure~\\ref{fig:process-kappa}), rising to $r = +0.24$ ($p < 0.001$) on Qwen2.5-14B. This pattern generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the cosine--$\\kappa$ gap narrows from $0.23$ (Mistral) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), meaning answer-level $\\kappa$ already captures the bottleneck for strong models, while process-$\\kappa$ reveals hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. In all families, interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_process_kappa_scatter.pdf}\n\\vspace{-2mm}\n\\caption{Answer agreement vs.\\ CoT cosine similarity (Mistral-7B, FOLIO, $n{=}204$). Flat regression ($r = -0.06$, $p = 0.36$); stronger models show significant positive correlation ($r = +0.24$, Qwen-14B; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}).}\n\\label{fig:process-kappa}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics---constraint ablation, discriminativeness, entropy decomposition, and hyperparameter sensitivity---confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nWe test more than twenty remediation strategies spanning prompt engineering, method-level redesign, and ten additional directions. All produce neutral or negative results.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-level interventions.} Nine strategies spanning six dimensions (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:remediation}) all fail: contrastive prompting reduces non-discriminative constraints but $\\Delta = -1.1$\\,pp; cross-model extraction yields accuracy within $\\pm 1$\\,pp; multi-temperature ($T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$) yields $\\Delta = 0.00$\\,pp.\n\n\\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} ICG ($\\Delta \\approx 0$, $p = .206$), Contrastive ($\\Delta = -9.31$\\,pp, $p < 0.01$), and Debate ($\\Delta = -18.63$/$-23.00$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$) all fail; three of six conditions reach significance, all negative (Table~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, Appendix~\\ref{app:remediation}).\n\n\\paragraph{Additional directions.} Ten further experiments---code verification, iterative refinement, logprob filtering, cross-model ensembles, alternative objectives---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:additional_interventions}).\n\n\\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} (Appendix) compares $\\kappa$ across four aggregation methods and three model families. SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$; Debate reaches $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp. All families cluster above $\\kappa = 0.40$, confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa.",

    "oldString": "On FOLIO, the gains show per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ SC's 38.10\\%) via contradiction detection, while SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%); when both agree, accuracy reaches 68.48\\% ($+14.07$\\,pp).",
    "newString": "On FOLIO, this produces per-label complementarity precisely where the LLM is weakest: \\textsc{False} problems constitute 43\\% of joint errors (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), and NLI excels exactly there (71.43\\% vs.\\ SC's 38.10\\%) via contradiction detection, while SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\nThe analyses in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}--\\S\\ref{sec:remediation} confirm all three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}): self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's bias and cannot break the same-model ceiling. The oracle gap (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms the aggregation mechanism is sound given uncontaminated constraints; the corrective signal must come from outside the model's distribution---escaping the mirage entirely.\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test this with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M parameters, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}. For each problem, DeBERTa classifies the (premise, conclusion) pair into \\textsc{entailment}, \\textsc{contradiction}, or \\textsc{neutral}. In combo mode, the NLI verdict is integrated with SC's $K$ votes via a weight parameter $w$: each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), because NLI classification requires (premise, conclusion) pairs that these datasets naturally provide; multiple-choice datasets (LogiQA) lack this structure.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\nDeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode at $w{=}1$ (NLI as 13th voter) reaches 57.84\\% ($\\Delta = +1.96$\\,pp, McNemar exact $p = 0.125$, n.s.; $b{=}0$, $c{=}4$); $w{=}3$ yields 58.82\\% ($+2.94$\\,pp, $p = 0.210$, n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}1$ yields $+0.83$\\,pp (n.s.), $w{=}3$ achieves $+3.50$\\,pp ($p = 0.013$), and $w{=}5$ reaches $+10.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$). Notably, same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter performance ($-2.83$\\,pp, $p = 0.014$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), while the cross-architecture combo yields significant improvement---illustrating the Symbolic Mirage prediction (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}) that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution. We report $w{=}3$ on ProofWriter as the primary finding; higher weights risk overfitting. After Benjamini--Hochberg correction for six comparisons ($m{=}6$), ProofWriter $w{=}3$ remains significant (adjusted $p = 0.038$) and $w{=}5$ (adjusted $p < 0.001$); all four FOLIO comparisons are non-significant. A per-label caveat: DeBERTa over-predicts \\textsc{False} on ProofWriter (67.8\\% of predictions) and performs poorly on \\textsc{Unknown} (3.5\\% accuracy), mirroring the FOLIO pattern where NLI's ``neutral'' fails to capture logical unknowability.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nThe 406M encoder-only DeBERTa and 7B decoder-only Mistral share no architecture, training data, or inference mechanism, so they err on different subsets---satisfying the independence condition that same-model sampling cannot provide (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). On FOLIO, the gains show per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ SC's 38.10\\%) via contradiction detection, while SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%); when both agree, accuracy reaches 68.48\\% ($+14.07$\\,pp).\n",

We report $w{=}3$ on ProofWriter as the primary finding; higher weights risk overfitting. [Director 指令] [Patch] Dir-C 204 全量结果出来了，需整合到论文：

**Dir-C 204 结果**: Premise-Only SICA = 56.86% (+2.45pp vs SC), McNemar p=0.359 → 不显著。
但有重要诊断发现：过滤掉 23% 的 conclusion-referencing constraints 后，收益减半 → **conclusion-bias 贡献约 50% 的 constraint error**。

**整合位置**: Section 4/5 Analysis — 作为 Symbolic Confirmation Bias 的量化证据：
- "约 50% 的约束错误来源于 conclusion-referencing constraints"
- 这与 BR=4.97× 互补：bias ratio 量化了方向性偏差强度，conclusion-bias 量化了偏差来源比例

**同时更新 Method Exhaustion Table**:
| Method | Δ vs SC | p-value | Verdict |
|--------|---------|---------|---------|
| A: Atom Pipeline | -10pp | — | Negative |
| B: Weighted MAX-SAT | +1pp | n.s. | Negative |
| C: Premise-Only Filter | +2.45pp | 0.359 | Not significant |
| D: DPP Trace Selection | +1.47pp (K=8) | 0.629 | Not significant |
| R: Cross-Arch NLI (DeBERTa) | +3.50pp (PW) | 0.013* | **Positive (BH-confirmed)** |

5/6 同模型方法失败，唯一成功的是跨架构方法 → 直接支持 Symbolic Mirage 核心论点。

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01RZH8WU8BqNjYiWPxchKMGh`</summary>

```text
40	
41	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
42	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
43	
44	The trace correlation above manifests through a specific mechanism that explains \emph{all three} regime failures: self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\times$ (pooled raw ratio for Mistral-7B FOLIO default seed; $4.24\times$ when MaxSAT-weighted; per-model ratios in Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}) compared to correct problems. Consequently, 94\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors: symbolic aggregation adds no independent corrective signal. The MAX-SAT solver rejects only 3.4\% of constraints on average ($|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| / |\mathcal{C}|$, $p = 0.636$ between correct/incorrect), confirming degeneration (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). Counterintuitively, jointly-wrong problems produce \emph{more} constraints (41.9 vs.\ 37.1 for correct): higher symbolic density amplifies rather than corrects bias.
45	
46	The causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts FOL consistent with it. Constraints from majority-answer traces use trace-specific variable names (56.4\% of cross-answer pairs share zero Z3 variables; Z3 operates over logical structure via alpha-equivalence, mitigating this syntactic confound), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all, and aggregation reduces to a weighted majority vote (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). Confirmation bias generalizes across model families: bias ratios of $4.97\times$ (Mistral-7B), $3.93\times$ (Qwen2.5-14B), and $2.88\times$ (LLaMA-3.1-8B) all significantly exceed $1.0$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). The bias ratio gradient aligns with regime boundaries: high bias (Mistral, $4.24\times$ weighted) at intermediate SC produces SICA's only positive trends; low bias (LLaMA, $2.66\times$) at high SC produces the worst degradation ($-4.41$\,pp). Error analysis reveals 64\% (52/81) of joint SICA+SC errors predict \textsc{Unknown} for ground-truth \textsc{True}/\textsc{False} problems---a systematic ``hedge to Unknown'' avoidance strategy---and 30\% (24/81) are \emph{confident systematic errors} where all 12 traces agree on the wrong answer. \textsc{False} problems are disproportionately hard: 43\% of joint errors vs.\ 21\% of correct predictions (Appendix~\ref{app:error-categorization}). The oracle ceiling (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms that ${\sim}$88\% of the same-model potential gain is lost to self-extraction quality.
47	
48	
49	\subsection{Process-Level Independence}
```

```text
73	\subsection{Remediation Analysis}
74	\label{sec:remediation}
75	\label{sec:interventions}
76	
77	We test more than twenty remediation strategies spanning prompt engineering, method-level redesign, and ten additional directions. All produce neutral or negative results.
78	
79	\paragraph{Prompt-level interventions.} Nine strategies spanning six dimensions (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:remediation}) all fail: contrastive prompting reduces non-discriminative constraints but $\Delta = -1.1$\,pp; cross-model extraction yields accuracy within $\pm 1$\,pp; multi-temperature ($T \in \{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\}$) yields $\Delta = 0.00$\,pp.
80	
81	\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} ICG ($\Delta \approx 0$, $p = .206$), Contrastive ($\Delta = -9.31$\,pp, $p < 0.01$), and Debate ($\Delta = -18.63$/$-23.00$\,pp, $p < 0.001$) all fail; three of six conditions reach significance, all negative (Table~\ref{tab:method_interventions}, Appendix~\ref{app:remediation}).
82	
83	\paragraph{Additional directions.} Ten further experiments---code verification, iterative refinement, logprob filtering, cross-model ensembles, alternative objectives---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:additional_interventions}).
84	
85	\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} (Appendix) compares $\kappa$ across four aggregation methods and three model families. SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$; Debate reaches $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp. All families cluster above $\kappa = 0.40$, confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa.
```

</details>

421	\label{app:additional-interventions}

424	\centering

426	\caption{Additional intervention results beyond the nine prompt-level and three method-level strategies reported in the main text. All produce neutral-to-negative results, completing the exhaustive remediation analysis across 22+ distinct approaches.}
427	\label{tab:additional_interventions}
428	\begin{tabular}{@{}llllrl@{}}
429	\toprule
430	\# & Method & Type & Target & $\Delta$ (pp) & Key Finding \\
431	\midrule
432	13 & PROVE (code verif.) & Paradigm & Formal proof & $-$1.9 & 15.2\% compilable; conclusive acc.\ = 50\% \\
433	14 & CEGIS (iterative) & Multi-round & Consistency & 0.0$^\dagger$ & R1 $+$3.43\,pp then oscillates \\
434	15 & DeepConf (logprob) & Filtering & Confidence & 0.0 & $\Delta_{\text{logprob}} = 0.006$ (no separation) \\
435	16 & Cross-model SICA & Cross-model & Independence & $-$0.49 & $\kappa_{\text{cross}}{=}0.42$ vs $\kappa_{\text{within}}{=}0.53$ \\
436	17 & Problem perturbation & Input diversity & Trace diversity & $<$0 & $\kappa$ \emph{increased} to 0.70 (anti-effect) \\
437	18 & MIN-UNSAT & Solver objective & Constraint sel.\ & $-$6.5 & Mean excluded constraints $= 1.3$ \\
438	19 & $\kappa$-aware SC & Voting rule & Weighted voting & 0.0 & 11/11 conditions $\Delta = 0$ \\
439	20 & Z3 trace filter & Trace filtering & Quality & $-$0.49 & Precision 49.1\%, 7 questions affected \\
440	21 & Bootstrapped gold & Iterative & Self-improvement & 0.0$^\dagger$ & R1 $+$2.45\,pp then oscillates \\
441	22 & Selective abstention & Diagnostic & Confidence & $+$4.4$^*$ & AUROC $= 0.673$, at 80\% coverage \\
442	23 & ASCII perturbation & Input diversity & Format diversity & 0.0 & $\kappa$: $0.39 \to 0.36$ (negligible) \\
443	\bottomrule
444	\end{tabular}\\[2pt]
445	{\footnotesize All experiments on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n{=}204$) unless noted. $^\dagger$\,Oscillates without convergence; net effect $\approx 0$. $^*$\,Accuracy gain only at reduced coverage (diagnostic value, not a general improvement).     "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nThis section tests the three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}): trace correlation and $K_{\\text{eff}}$ collapse (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), systematic confirmation bias with $\\text{BR} > 1$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}), supporting diagnostics (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostics}), and the inescapability of the mirage under twenty-plus same-model interventions (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$, yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence alone, appears to influence the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ produces significant degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$; positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\nThe trace correlation above manifests through a specific mechanism that explains \\emph{all three} regime failures: self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\\times$ (pooled raw ratio for Mistral-7B FOLIO default seed; $4.24\\times$ when MaxSAT-weighted; per-model ratios in Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}) compared to correct problems. Consequently, 94\\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors: symbolic aggregation adds no independent corrective signal. The MAX-SAT solver rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints on average ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| / |\\mathcal{C}|$, $p = 0.636$ between correct/incorrect), confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). Counterintuitively, jointly-wrong problems produce \\emph{more} constraints (41.9 vs.\\ 37.1 for correct): higher symbolic density amplifies rather than corrects bias.\n\nThe causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts FOL consistent with it. Constraints from majority-answer traces use trace-specific variable names (56.4\\% of cross-answer pairs share zero Z3 variables; Z3 operates over logical structure via alpha-equivalence, mitigating this syntactic confound), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all, and aggregation reduces to a weighted majority vote (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). Confirmation bias generalizes across model families: bias ratios of $4.97\\times$ (Mistral-7B), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen2.5-14B), and $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA-3.1-8B) all significantly exceed $1.0$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). The bias ratio gradient aligns with regime boundaries: high bias (Mistral, $4.24\\times$ weighted) at intermediate SC produces SICA's only positive trends; low bias (LLaMA, $2.66\\times$) at high SC produces the worst degradation ($-4.41$\\,pp). Error analysis reveals 64\\% (52/81) of joint SICA+SC errors predict \\textsc{Unknown} for ground-truth \\textsc{True}/\\textsc{False} problems---a systematic ``hedge to Unknown'' avoidance strategy---and 30\\% (24/81) are \\emph{confident systematic errors} where all 12 traces agree on the wrong answer. \\textsc{False} problems are disproportionately hard: 43\\% of joint errors vs.\\ 21\\% of correct predictions (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}). The oracle ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms that ${\\sim}$88\\% of the same-model potential gain is lost to self-extraction quality.\n\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nAnswer-level $\\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.41$. The process--answer correlation is model-strength dependent: $r = -0.06$ ($p = 0.36$) on Mistral-7B, where answer diversity does not reflect reasoning diversity (Figure~\\ref{fig:process-kappa}), rising to $r = +0.24$ ($p < 0.001$) on Qwen2.5-14B. This pattern generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the cosine--$\\kappa$ gap narrows from $0.23$ (Mistral) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), meaning answer-level $\\kappa$ already captures the bottleneck for strong models, while process-$\\kappa$ reveals hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. In all families, interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_process_kappa_scatter.pdf}\n\\vspace{-2mm}\n\\caption{Answer agreement vs.\\ CoT cosine similarity (Mistral-7B, FOLIO, $n{=}204$). Flat regression ($r = -0.06$, $p = 0.36$); stronger models show significant positive correlation ($r = +0.24$, Qwen-14B; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}).}\n\\label{fig:process-kappa}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics---constraint ablation, discriminativeness, entropy decomposition, and hyperparameter sensitivity---confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nWe test more than twenty remediation strategies spanning prompt engineering, method-level redesign, and ten additional directions. All produce neutral or negative results.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-level interventions.} Nine strategies spanning six dimensions (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:remediation}) all fail: contrastive prompting reduces non-discriminative constraints but $\\Delta = -1.1$\\,pp; cross-model extraction yields accuracy within $\\pm 1$\\,pp; multi-temperature ($T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$) yields $\\Delta = 0.00$\\,pp.\n\n\\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} ICG ($\\Delta \\approx 0$, $p = .206$), Contrastive ($\\Delta = -9.31$\\,pp, $p < 0.01$), and Debate ($\\Delta = -18.63$/$-23.00$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$) all fail; three of six conditions reach significance, all negative (Table~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, Appendix~\\ref{app:remediation}).\n\n\\paragraph{Additional directions.} Ten further experiments---code verification, iterative refinement, logprob filtering, cross-model ensembles, alternative objectives---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:additional_interventions}).\n\n\\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} (Appendix) compares $\\kappa$ across four aggregation methods and three model families. SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$; Debate reaches $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp. All families cluster above $\\kappa = 0.40$, confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa.",

The oracle ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms that ${\\sim}$88\\% of the same-model potential gain is lost to self-extraction quality.",

{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "cf0ffb1a-f183-457f-8252-c3edbf065b99",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "221c046d-38a1-4615-a8a2-6bda0aab5278",
  "type": "user",
  "uuid": "75ef301f-67f6-48eb-af38-eb9ff4a3df8e",
  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/analysis.tex",
    "oldString": "\\paragraph{Additional directions.} Ten further experiments---code verification, iterative refinement, logprob filtering, cross-model ensembles, alternative objectives---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:additional_interventions}).",
    "newString": "\\paragraph{Additional directions.} Thirteen further experiments---code verification, iterative refinement, logprob filtering, cross-model ensembles, premise-only filtering, DPP trace selection, and alternative objectives---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:additional_interventions}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nThis section tests the three predictions of the Symbolic Mirage hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}): trace correlation and $K_{\\text{eff}}$ collapse (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), systematic confirmation bias with $\\text{BR} > 1$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}), process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}), supporting diagnostics (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostics}), and the inescapability of the mirage under twenty-plus same-model interventions (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. The four Mistral-7B FOLIO seeds produce $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$, yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp---seed-level constraint quality, not independence alone, appears to influence the variation. Two conditions are jointly necessary for positive $\\Delta$: sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ produces significant degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-kappa}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$; positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\nThe trace correlation above manifests through a specific mechanism that explains \\emph{all three} regime failures: self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\\times$ (pooled raw ratio for Mistral-7B FOLIO default seed; $4.24\\times$ when MaxSAT-weighted; per-model ratios in Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}) compared to correct problems. Consequently, 94\\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors: symbolic aggregation adds no independent corrective signal. The MAX-SAT solver rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints on average ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| / |\\mathcal{C}|$, $p = 0.636$ between correct/incorrect), confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). Counterintuitively, jointly-wrong problems produce \\emph{more} constraints (41.9 vs.\\ 37.1 for correct): higher symbolic density amplifies rather than corrects bias.\n\nThe causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts FOL consistent with it. Constraints use trace-specific variable names (56.4\\% zero cross-answer variable overlap), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all and aggregation reduces to a weighted majority vote (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). A premise-only experiment isolates the bias source: filtering out the 23\\% of constraints that reference the conclusion halves the accuracy gain ($+2.45$\\,pp, $p = 0.359$, n.s.), indicating that conclusion-referencing constraints account for approximately half of the constraint error. Confirmation bias generalizes across model families: bias ratios of $4.97\\times$ (Mistral-7B), $3.93\\times$ (Qwen2.5-14B), and $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA-3.1-8B) all significantly exceed $1.0$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). The bias ratio gradient aligns with regime boundaries: high bias (Mistral, $4.24\\times$ weighted) at intermediate SC produces SICA's only positive trends; low bias (LLaMA, $2.66\\times$) at high SC produces the worst degradation ($-4.41$\\,pp). Error analysis reveals 64\\% (52/81) of joint SICA+SC errors predict \\textsc{Unknown} for ground-truth \\textsc{True}/\\textsc{False} problems---a systematic ``hedge to Unknown'' avoidance strategy---and 30\\% (24/81) are \\emph{confident systematic errors} where all 12 traces agree on the wrong answer. \\textsc{False} problems are disproportionately hard: 43\\% of joint errors vs.\\ 21\\% of correct predictions (Appendix~\\ref{app:error-categorization}). The oracle ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms that ${\\sim}$88\\% of the same-model potential gain is lost to self-extraction quality.\n\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nAnswer-level $\\kappa$ may underestimate the independence bottleneck: two traces can follow nearly identical reasoning yet reach different final answers, causing $\\kappa$ to treat them as disagreeing. We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), mean ProcessSim is $0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.41$. The process--answer correlation is model-strength dependent: $r = -0.06$ ($p = 0.36$) on Mistral-7B, where answer diversity does not reflect reasoning diversity (Figure~\\ref{fig:process-kappa}), rising to $r = +0.24$ ($p < 0.001$) on Qwen2.5-14B. This pattern generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}): the cosine--$\\kappa$ gap narrows from $0.23$ (Mistral) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), meaning answer-level $\\kappa$ already captures the bottleneck for strong models, while process-$\\kappa$ reveals hidden homogeneity in weaker models where answer diversity masks process convergence. In all families, interventions targeting answer diversity (temperature scaling, diverse prompting) cannot alter the underlying reasoning paths.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_process_kappa_scatter.pdf}\n\\vspace{-2mm}\n\\caption{Answer agreement vs.\\ CoT cosine similarity (Mistral-7B, FOLIO, $n{=}204$). Flat regression ($r = -0.06$, $p = 0.36$); stronger models show significant positive correlation ($r = +0.24$, Qwen-14B; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}).}\n\\label{fig:process-kappa}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics---constraint ablation, discriminativeness, entropy decomposition, and hyperparameter sensitivity---confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\nWe test more than twenty remediation strategies spanning prompt engineering, method-level redesign, and ten additional directions. All produce neutral or negative results.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-level interventions.} Nine strategies spanning six dimensions (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:remediation}) all fail: contrastive prompting reduces non-discriminative constraints but $\\Delta = -1.1$\\,pp; cross-model extraction yields accuracy within $\\pm 1$\\,pp; multi-temperature ($T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$) yields $\\Delta = 0.00$\\,pp.\n\n\\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} ICG ($\\Delta \\approx 0$, $p = .206$), Contrastive ($\\Delta = -9.31$\\,pp, $p < 0.01$), and Debate ($\\Delta = -18.63$/$-23.00$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$) all fail; three of six conditions reach significance, all negative (Table~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, Appendix~\\ref{app:remediation}).\n\n\\paragraph{Additional directions.} Ten further experiments---code verification, iterative refinement, logprob filtering, cross-model ensembles, alternative objectives---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:additional_interventions}).\n\n\\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} (Appendix) compares $\\kappa$ across four aggregation methods and three model families. SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$; Debate reaches $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp. All families cluster above $\\kappa = 0.40$, confirming a same-model ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and vice versa.",
    "structuredPatch": [

```text
83	\end{figure}
84	
85	\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}
86	\label{sec:mirage}
87	
88	We hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. This \textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\S\ref{sec:oracle}). We quantify mirage intensity via the \emph{Bias Ratio}:
89	\begin{equation}
90	\label{eq:mirage}
91	\text{BR}(M,T) = \frac{P(c \text{ supports } a_M \mid a_M \neq a^*)}{P(c \text{ supports } a^* \mid a_M \neq a^*)},
92	\end{equation}
93	where $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. $\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints project model bias rather than provide independent verification. The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}; Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). Together with the oracle gap (\S\ref{sec:oracle}), BR bounds improvement: self-extracted constraints capture at most a $1/\text{BR}$ fraction of the oracle ceiling ($\Delta_{\max} \lesssim 6.0$\,pp for Mistral-7B, $\lesssim 3.6$\,pp for Qwen-14B; observed mean across conditions: $+2.12$\,pp). Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below $1.0$ (\S\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
94	
```

    "originalFile": "\\section{Background \\& Setup}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}\n\\label{sec:condorcet}\n\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \\to \\infty$ \\citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \\citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \\citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:effk}\nK_{\\text{eff}} = \\frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \\cdot \\kappa}.\n\\end{equation}\nIn our experiments (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2\\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. Note that $\\kappa$ is a qualitative proxy for the intraclass correlation coefficient (ICC); we use $K_{\\text{eff}}$ as an interpretive indicator rather than a precise statistical estimator. This \\emph{independence bottleneck} motivates our investigation.\n\n\\subsection{SICA Pipeline}\n\\label{sec:pipeline}\n\nSymbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\n\n\\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\\{t_1, \\ldots, t_K\\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.\n\n\\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts FOL constraints from each trace at $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompts}). The \\emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence; each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:maxsat}\n\\mathcal{C}^* = \\arg\\max_{\\mathcal{S} \\subseteq \\mathcal{C}} \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{S}} w_c \\quad \\text{s.t.} \\quad \\mathcal{S} \\text{ is satisfiable.}\n\\end{equation}\nZ3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\\texttt{answer\\_a} == \\texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.\n\n\\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus} = \\mathcal{C} \\setminus \\mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:score}\n\\text{score}(a) = \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^*} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a) - \\alpha \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \\arg\\max_{a} \\text{score}(a)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:degenerate}\n\\text{score}(a) \\approx \\sum_{\\substack{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^* \\\\ \\text{source}(c) \\to a}} w_c,\n\\end{equation}\nequivalent to majority voting. We show in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.\n\n\\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\\{q : |\\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\\}| / |\\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic computable before extraction. When $C_0 < 5\\%$, SICA produces the same output as SC. The penalty weight $\\alpha = 0.5$ is not a critical hyperparameter: accuracy varies by at most $0.49$\\,pp across $\\alpha \\in \\{0.0, 0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1.0\\}$ on Mistral-7B FOLIO.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.4cm and 0.35cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.6cm,\n                text width=1.6cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract FOL\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts FOL constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}\n\\label{sec:mirage}\n\nWe hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. This \\textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}). We quantify mirage intensity via the \\emph{Bias Ratio}:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:mirage}\n\\text{BR}(M,T) = \\frac{P(c \\text{ supports } a_M \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)}{P(c \\text{ supports } a^* \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)},\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. $\\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints project model bias rather than provide independent verification. The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). Together with the oracle gap (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}), BR bounds improvement: self-extracted constraints capture at most a $1/\\text{BR}$ fraction of the oracle ceiling ($\\Delta_{\\max} \\lesssim 6.0$\\,pp for Mistral-7B, $\\lesssim 3.6$\\,pp for Qwen-14B; observed mean across conditions: $+2.12$\\,pp). Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below $1.0$ (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n",

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The Director is orchestrating a Paper Agent to finalize an EMNLP 2026 ARR paper (8pp main text limit) about SICA and why self-extracted symbolic constraints fail in LLM reasoning. This session covered:
   - Reporting V2 framing execution completion to Director
   - Acknowledging V3 rollback and emergency constraints (D095)
   - Executing D096: Discussion expansion (≥250 words, 3 paragraphs) + Conclusion rewrite (~100 words)
   - Executing D097: Symbolic Mirage unified theory reframe across all sections
   - Integrating Director's formal theoretical framework (Definition + Propositions 1-4)
   - Executing Batch 1 text fixes (W10/W6/W7/W14/Q5/Q6/W3 + trimming)
   - Executing Batch 2 (W1 title change, introduction verification, Director's compression edits confirmed)
   - Round 6 Final Data Integration (BH correction, TOST, α sensitivity, constraint rejection, pairwise correlation, Lakens citation)
   - Dir-G Error Analysis integration (constraint density paradox, error taxonomy, gold label skew)
   - Dir-C Premise-Only results integration (conclusion-bias 50%, appendix table rows #24-26)
   - Dir-H Provenance Bias Gradient (prediction 4 added to §3.3, empirical evidence pending for §5.2)
   - Dir-E Z3 Strict-Mode (pending appendix table addition)

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis**: Self-extracted symbolic structures are projections of model bias, not independent verification. BR(M,T) = P(c supports a_M | a_M ≠ a*) / P(c supports a* | a_M ≠ a*). BR > 1 = mirage present.
   - **Empirical Accuracy Bound**: Δ_max ≤ Oracle_Gap / BR ≈ 6.0pp Mistral, 3.6pp Qwen-14B; observed mean +2.12pp
   - **Four testable predictions**: (1) BR > 1.0 across architectures, (2) no same-model intervention reduces BR below 1.0, (3) only external verification escapes mirage, (4) constraint bias increases along reasoning steps
   - **TOST equivalence**: 4/15 equivalence, 5/15 genuine difference (4 negative), 6/15 underpowered
   - **BH correction**: ProofWriter w=3 adjusted p=0.038, w=5 adjusted p<0.001; FOLIO comparisons n.s.
   - **Conclusion-bias**: 23% conclusion-referencing constraints account for ~50% of constraint error
   - **Provenance Bias Gradient**: Late-step (7+) 27.23% leakage vs early (1-3) 22.00%, Fisher p=0.000003; late-only SICA 44.12% (-10.29pp), early-only 56.37% (+1.96pp)
   - **Error Taxonomy**: 64% hedge-to-Unknown, 30% confident systematic errors, False 43% of errors
   - **Constraint density paradox**: both_wrong 41.9 constraints vs both_correct 37.1
   - **Cross-architecture**: DeBERTa-large NLI primary finding +3.50pp ProofWriter w=3 p=0.013 (BH-confirmed)
   - **EMNLP 2026 ARR**: 8-page main text limit, Limitations don't count

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **main.tex** (644, was 444)
     - Title changed from "Fail" to "Do Not Improve"
     ```latex
     \title{Beyond Self-Consistency: Why Self-Extracted Symbolic Constraints\\Do Not Improve LLM Reasoning}

        "content": "\\section{Background \\& Setup}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Self-Consistency and the Condorcet Independence Assumption}\n\\label{sec:condorcet}\n\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. The Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees that if $K$ independent voters are each correct with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth as $K \\to \\infty$ \\citep{austensmith1996}. LLM traces violate this independence: all $K$ samples come from the same model with only temperature as variation. Following \\citet{ladha1992correlated} and the design-effect framework of \\citet{kish1965survey}, we define effective sample size using Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa} as proxy for intraclass correlation:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:effk}\nK_{\\text{eff}} = \\frac{K}{1 + (K - 1) \\cdot \\kappa}.\n\\end{equation}\nIn our experiments (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}), $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across instruction-tuned pairs (0.91 for thinking mode), yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} \\approx 1.2\\text{--}3.6$ for $K = 12$. Note that $\\kappa$ is a qualitative proxy for the intraclass correlation coefficient (ICC); we use $K_{\\text{eff}}$ as an interpretive indicator rather than a precise statistical estimator. This \\emph{independence bottleneck} motivates our investigation.\n\n\\subsection{SICA Pipeline}\n\\label{sec:pipeline}\n\nSymbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\n\n\\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\\{t_1, \\ldots, t_K\\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.\n\n\\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts FOL constraints from each trace at $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompts}). The \\emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence; each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:maxsat}\n\\mathcal{C}^* = \\arg\\max_{\\mathcal{S} \\subseteq \\mathcal{C}} \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{S}} w_c \\quad \\text{s.t.} \\quad \\mathcal{S} \\text{ is satisfiable.}\n\\end{equation}\nZ3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\\texttt{answer\\_a} == \\texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.\n\n\\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus} = \\mathcal{C} \\setminus \\mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:score}\n\\text{score}(a) = \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^*} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a) - \\alpha \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \\arg\\max_{a} \\text{score}(a)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:degenerate}\n\\text{score}(a) \\approx \\sum_{\\substack{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^* \\\\ \\text{source}(c) \\to a}} w_c,\n\\end{equation}\nequivalent to majority voting. We show in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.\n\n\\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\\{q : |\\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\\}| / |\\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic computable before extraction. When $C_0 < 5\\%$, SICA produces the same output as SC. The penalty weight $\\alpha = 0.5$ is not a critical hyperparameter: accuracy varies by at most $0.49$\\,pp across $\\alpha \\in \\{0.0, 0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1.0\\}$ on Mistral-7B FOLIO.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.4cm and 0.35cm,\n    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.6cm,\n                text width=1.6cm, align=center, font=\\scriptsize},\n    arr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick},\n    darr/.style={-{Stealth[length=4pt]}, thick, densely dashed},\n    lbl/.style={font=\\scriptsize, text=gray!70!black},\n    ibottle/.style={draw=red!70!black, densely dashed, rounded corners=3pt,\n                    inner sep=3pt},\n]\n\\node[box] (q) {Question $q$};\n\\node[box, right=of q] (sample) {Sample $K$\\\\traces};\n\\node[box, right=of sample] (extract) {Extract FOL\\\\constraints};\n\\node[box, right=of extract] (dedup) {Dedup.\\ +\\\\weight};\n\\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\\\(Z3)};\n\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of maxsat, fill=gray!12] (ans) {Answer $a^*$};\n\\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\\\vote};\n\n\\draw[arr] (q) -- (sample);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- (extract);\n\\draw[arr] (extract) -- (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- ++(0,-0.35) node[lbl, below, yshift=-0.05cm] {oracle: gold FOL};\n\\end{tikzpicture}\n\\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts FOL constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}\n\\label{sec:mirage}\n\nWe hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. This \\textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}). We quantify mirage intensity via the \\emph{Bias Ratio}:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:mirage}\n\\text{BR}(M,T) = \\frac{P(c \\text{ supports } a_M \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)}{P(c \\text{ supports } a^* \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)},\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. $\\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints project model bias rather than provide independent verification. The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\\text{eff}} < 2$ (\\emph{direct}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}). Together with the oracle gap (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}), BR bounds improvement: self-extracted constraints capture at most a $1/\\text{BR}$ fraction of the oracle ceiling ($\\Delta_{\\max} \\lesssim 6.0$\\,pp for Mistral-7B, $\\lesssim 3.6$\\,pp for Qwen-14B; observed mean across conditions: $+2.12$\\,pp). Four testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below $1.0$ (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}); (4)~constraint bias increases along reasoning steps, as later steps increasingly condition on the model's emerging answer.\n",
        "numLines": 94,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 94
      }
